[
  {
    "role": "system",
    "content": "You are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF20.9, F25.9, F33.3, F32.3, F06.8, F31.9\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, it’s good to meet you. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess… the main thing is I’ve just gotten really slow, like noticeably slow. I do hair, and at work it’s like my hands don’t do what they used to do. Even little things that were automatic before—sectioning someone’s hair, getting foils ready, mixing color—feel like I’m moving my hands through glue or something. Like there’s this drag on everything.\n\nAnd it’s not just me being picky about my work. Other people have started noticing too, which is honestly embarrassing. I used to be pretty quick, or at least normal, and now I’ll realize I’m taking forever on something simple. I’ll be standing there trying to get through a service, and it feels like my body is just… delayed. That’s kind of what made me think I should come in, because it’s affecting my job and people can tell I’m not moving like I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any shaking or tremor in your hands?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a shaking, exactly. I haven’t noticed my hands trembling the way I’d imagine a tremor would look. It’s more that they feel slow or heavy when I’m trying to use them, but I can’t say I’ve seen them visibly shake. If there is any tiny movement, I haven’t really picked up on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. Pretty much most days lately, honestly. It’s not even always like crying or some big dramatic thing, it’s more like I wake up and there’s already this heaviness there, like this flat sadness sitting in my chest before anything has even happened. \n\nAnd then I start thinking about my life, and it’s like… I had this picture of what I was trying to build, you know? Work, getting better at hair, maybe having my own place feel more settled, just normal things. But lately it feels like there’s no point in trying to build any of it, like I’m already behind or like it’s not going to turn into anything. It’s hard to explain without sounding really negative, but that’s how it feels most mornings.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing or seeing things that other people don’t seem to?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah. Not seeing things, really, I don’t think. But hearing, yes… sometimes. I feel weird saying that out loud because I know how it sounds, but it’s happened enough that I can’t just pretend it’s nothing.\n\nIt’s mostly at work, like when I’m doing the boring cleanup stuff after a client—sweeping up hair, rinsing color bowls, wiping things down. I’ll be near the shampoo stations, and I’ll hear this woman’s voice, like she’s close by, saying that I’m ruining everything. It’s not like a thought in my own head exactly… it sounds more like someone nearby said it, so I’ll turn around or look over, but sometimes the salon is empty, or nobody is anywhere near me.\n\nAnd it’s embarrassing because I’ll kind of freeze for a second and check if someone came in or if one of the other girls said something, but there won’t be anyone there. It makes me feel really unsettled, like I don’t trust the room for a minute. It’s not happening every second or anything, but it’s happened more than once.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt unusually high, energized, or wired for days in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve had something like that in the way you mean, like being super energized or wired for days where I’m unstoppable or anything.\n\nI mean, there are times I can’t sleep well or my mind feels kind of busy, but I wouldn’t say I feel “high.” It’s not like I suddenly have tons of energy and feel amazing. If anything, lately I usually feel kind of weighed down. So… no, I don’t think I’ve had days in a row where I felt unusually up or wired.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard that woman’s voice at times when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, yes, there was a stretch before the depression got as heavy as it is now where I was still kind of functioning normally. Like I was getting up, going to work, doing clients, keeping up with things more or less. I didn’t feel great, exactly, but it wasn’t that same heavy, hopeless feeling taking over every day yet.\n\nBut during that time, I was already hearing her voice sometimes. And it kind of went along with this feeling I had at the salon, like someone there was messing with my schedule or interfering with it somehow. I remember feeling really sure about it, like shifts or appointments were being moved around or handled in a way that was meant to throw me off. And the voice would make it feel even more real, because I’d hear her saying things and then I’d start thinking, “Okay, see, something is happening here.”\n\nSo… yeah. It wasn’t only when I was depressed. It started, or at least it was happening, before the depression really took over.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least 2 weeks when you were hearing the woman’s voice even though you were not feeling depressed most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think it was definitely at least two weeks. It wasn’t just like one weird day or a couple of days. Before the depression really got bad, there was this stretch of several weeks where I was still mostly doing my normal routine—getting up, going into the salon, doing clients, cleaning up, answering people, like… functioning more normally, I guess.\n\nI wasn’t feeling happy, exactly, but I also wasn’t spending most of the time in that really heavy depressed place yet. And during that time, I was already hearing her voice sometimes. It would happen at work, and then I’d start connecting it with things at the salon, like my schedule or appointments feeling like they were being messed with. I remember feeling pretty sure someone there was interfering with it, like not just a mistake, but on purpose.\n\nSo, yeah… if you mean hearing the voice while I wasn’t depressed most of the time, then yes. There was a period like that, and it lasted several weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice and the feeling that people at the salon were interfering with your schedule been going on for at least 6 months?\n\nPatient: Yes… it’s definitely been more than six months. It’s been, I think, about a year and four months now since the voice started happening and since I started feeling like someone was targeting my bookings or messing with my schedule at the salon.\n\nIt’s hard to say the exact date because it didn’t feel like one clear moment where everything suddenly started. It was more like little things at first, like I’d notice something off with the appointments or I’d hear her voice and then I’d start putting it together. But if I’m being honest, this has been going on for around a year and four months, not just a few months.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been doing noticeably worse overall at work or in taking care of yourself than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I mean, I don’t like admitting that, because I used to be really reliable at work. Like, I was the person who remembered little things for clients, kept my station clean, stayed on top of timing, all of that. But since this started, I’ve definitely been doing worse.\n\nAt the salon, I make mistakes with really basic routines now, things I shouldn’t mess up—like mixing up small steps, forgetting what I already set out, taking too long to finish things that used to feel automatic. I’m slower with clients, and then I get nervous because I can tell they notice. My manager has lost trust in me, I think. She doesn’t say it in a cruel way, but she checks on me more, or hesitates before giving me certain appointments, and that feels awful because I know I wasn’t like that before.\n\nAnd with clients too… some of them don’t book with me as much, or they seem less comfortable. I’ve canceled things I used to always handle, even simple appointments or helping with cleanup or extra tasks. I used to be dependable, and now I feel like people don’t really count on me the same way.\n\nOutside of work, I’ve pulled away from friends a lot. I don’t answer messages like I used to, and I don’t really go out. I also stopped showing up for the volunteer haircuts I used to do at the senior center, which I feel guilty about, because I actually cared about that. It’s just like… overall I’m not functioning the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself talking much less than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t know if I noticed it at first, because I’m still technically talking at work when I have to, like asking what someone wants done with their hair or answering a basic question. But compared to how I used to be, it’s really different.\n\nI used to chat with clients the whole appointment sometimes—about their kids, vacations, shows, just little normal salon stuff. I’d remember details and ask follow-up questions, and it came naturally. I also used to help the newer stylists, like if they were nervous or unsure about something, I’d step in and talk them through it. I liked being that person.\n\nNow I mostly keep my head down. I say what I need to say, but I don’t really start conversations. If a client is quiet, I’m almost relieved because then I don’t have to pretend I have that energy. And even with friends, I used to go hiking and talk for hours, or make plans, or just send dumb messages during the day. Now I avoid people more. It’s not that I hate them or anything, I just… I don’t have that spark in me. Talking feels like it takes effort I don’t really have.\n\nDoctor: Have other people commented that your face looks less expressive or that your voice sounds flatter than it used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if anyone has said it exactly like that, like “your face looks flat” or “your voice is monotone.” Not in those words, at least. But people have definitely noticed that I’m not as… animated, I guess.\n\nAt work, I used to be really chatty with clients. Like I’d smile more, ask them about their lives, remember little things, joke around a bit, and it felt natural. I’d also help the newer stylists and kind of talk them through things if they were nervous. Now I mostly keep my head down and just say what I have to say to get through the appointment. So even if nobody says, “your face looks less expressive,” I can tell they feel the difference.\n\nFriends have noticed too. I used to go hiking with them and talk a lot, or make plans and actually sound excited. Now I avoid people more, and I think I probably do sound flatter because I just don’t have that spark in me like I used to. It’s not like I’m trying to be cold or rude. It just takes so much effort to seem normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s kind of embarrassing to say because some of the things I used to enjoy were such normal parts of who I was, and now they feel like chores or like I’m watching somebody else do them from far away.\n\nAt work, I used to actually enjoy talking with clients. Not just the haircut part, but the whole back-and-forth—asking about their family, remembering some little thing they told me last time, joking around, making them feel comfortable. I liked helping the newer stylists too, like if someone was nervous or unsure, I’d step in and talk them through it. That used to make me feel useful, like I had a place there.\n\nNow I mostly just keep my head down and try to get through the appointment. I don’t really start conversations unless I have to. If someone wants to chat, I feel this pressure to respond the right way, and I just don’t have that same spark in me. It’s not that I suddenly dislike them, it’s more like the interest or energy that used to come naturally just isn’t there.\n\nAnd with friends, yeah… I used to go hiking and actually look forward to it. I’d talk the whole time, make plans, take pictures, send messages afterward. Now I avoid people a lot more. Even if someone invites me, I’ll put off answering because the idea of going and acting like myself feels exhausting. So yes, I’ve lost interest, or maybe lost the ability to feel interested the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I’ve had plenty of nights where I didn’t sleep well, or I stayed up too late and then had to drag myself through the next day, but that’s different from feeling full of energy.\n\nIf you mean like sleeping only a few hours and then waking up feeling amazing or unstoppable or like I didn’t need sleep at all… I don’t think so. At least nothing that stands out to me that way. Usually if I get less sleep, I feel it. I get tired, slower, more worn down. It’s not like I suddenly have all this extra energy.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a head injury, seizure, or another neurological illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve bumped my head like anyone has, probably, but not anything I’d call a real head injury or anything where I had to be treated for it.\n\nNo seizures that I know of. And I haven’t been told I have some neurological illness or anything like that. I guess I’m not totally sure what all counts as neurological, but nothing comes to mind—no diagnosis or major event like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using methamphetamine, cocaine, or any other drugs around the times when you hear the woman’s voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, no, not that I can connect to it. I haven’t noticed it being tied to anything like that.\n\nWhen it happens, I don’t sit there thinking, “Oh, this is because I took something,” or anything like that. I guess I’d say I’m not aware of using meth or cocaine around those times. I’m trying to think carefully because I don’t want to answer wrong, but there isn’t some pattern I can point to where I hear her voice after using drugs.\n\nI’m sorry if that’s not a very clear answer. It’s just… from what I can tell, no, I haven’t noticed drug use being part of when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting several days when you felt unusually euphoric or irritable?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I’ve definitely been irritable for several days before, but I don’t know if I’d call it unusual in the way you mean. Like, if I’m stressed or overwhelmed, I can get snappy or kind of on edge for a while, but it doesn’t feel like some totally different version of me, if that makes sense.\n\nAs far as euphoric… I don’t really think so. I don’t remember a stretch of several days where I was just unusually happy or high or anything like that. I’ve had good moods, obviously, and times where I felt better than usual, but not in a way that seemed extreme or like other people were concerned.\n\nI guess I’m trying to separate normal mood stuff from what you’re asking. If you mean several days where I was so happy or so irritable that it really stood out as abnormal, I can’t think of a clear time like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
  },

    "content": "Return the JSON now."

Now I avoid people more, and I think I probably do sound flatter because I just don’t have that spark in me like I used to. It’s not like I’m trying to be cold or rude. It just takes so much effort to seem normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s kind of embarrassing to say because some of the things I used to enjoy were such normal parts of who I was, and now they feel like chores or like I’m watching somebody else do them from far away.\n\nAt work, I used to actually enjoy talking with clients. Not just the haircut part, but the whole back-and-forth—asking about their family, remembering some little thing they told me last time, joking around, making them feel comfortable. I liked helping the newer stylists too, like if someone was nervous or unsure, I’d step in and talk them through it. That used to make me feel useful, like I had a place there.\n\nNow I mostly just keep my head down and try to get through the appointment. I don’t really start conversations unless I have to. If someone wants to chat, I feel this pressure to respond the right way, and I just don’t have that same spark in me. It’s not that I suddenly dislike them, it’s more like the interest or energy that used to come naturally just isn’t there.\n\nAnd with friends, yeah… I used to go hiking and actually look forward to it. I’d talk the whole time, make plans, take pictures, send messages afterward. Now I avoid people a lot more. Even if someone invites me, I’ll put off answering because the idea of going and acting like myself feels exhausting. So yes, I’ve lost interest, or maybe lost the ability to feel interested the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I’ve had plenty of nights where I didn’t sleep well, or I stayed up too late and then had to drag myself through the next day, but that’s different from feeling full of energy.\n\nIf you mean like sleeping only a few hours and then waking up feeling amazing or unstoppable or like I didn’t need sleep at all… I don’t think so. At least nothing that stands out to me that way. Usually if I get less sleep, I feel it. I get tired, slower, more worn down. It’s not like I suddenly have all this extra energy.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a head injury, seizure, or another neurological illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve bumped my head like anyone has, probably, but not anything I’d call a real head injury or anything where I had to be treated for it.\n\nNo seizures that I know of. I haven’t noticed it being tied to anything like that.\n\nWhen it happens, I don’t sit there thinking, “Oh, this is because I took something,” or anything like that. I guess I’d say I’m not aware of using meth or cocaine around those times. I’m trying to think carefully because I don’t want to answer wrong, but there isn’t some pattern I can point to where I hear her voice after using drugs.\n\nI’m sorry if that’s not a very clear answer. Mostly it’s about that… about me ruining things, or being worthless, or like I’m messing everything up. That’s the main thing I hear.

It’ll happen at work sometimes when I’m doing really normal stuff, like sweeping up hair or rinsing out color bowls, and I’ll hear this woman’s voice kind of near the shampoo stations. And it feels like it’s nearby, not like I’m just thinking it. She’ll say I’m ruining everything, or that I can’t do anything right. Sometimes the salon is empty, or there’s nobody close enough to be saying it, which is what makes it so upsetting.

I’m trying to think if she says other things… but honestly, I don’t think there’s a lot outside of that theme. It’s usually critical, like about me being worthless or messing everything up.

I’ll be near the shampoo stations, and I’ll hear this woman’s voice, like she’s close by, saying that I’m ruining everything. It’s not like a thought in my own head exactly… it sounds more like someone nearby said it, so I’ll turn around or look over, but sometimes the salon is empty, or nobody is anywhere near me.\n\nAnd it’s embarrassing because I’ll kind of freeze for a second and check if someone came in or if one of the other girls said something, but there won’t be anyone there. It makes me feel really unsettled, like I don’t trust the room for a minute. I don’t think I’ve had something like that in the way you mean, like being super energized or wired for days where I’m unstoppable or anything.\n\nI mean, there are times I can’t sleep well or my mind feels kind of busy, but I wouldn’t say I feel “high.” It’s not like I suddenly have tons of energy and feel amazing. I mean, yes, there was a stretch before the depression got as heavy as it is now where I was still kind of functioning normally. It started, or at least it was happening, before the depression really took over.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least 2 weeks when you were hearing the woman’s voice even though you were not feeling depressed most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think it was definitely at least two weeks. It wasn’t just like one weird day or a couple of days. Before the depression really got bad, there was this stretch of several weeks where I was still mostly doing my normal routine—getting up, going into the salon, doing clients, cleaning up, answering people, like… functioning more normally, I guess.\n\nI wasn’t feeling happy, exactly, but I also wasn’t spending most of the time in that really heavy depressed place yet. And during that time, I was already hearing her voice sometimes. It would happen at work, and then I’d start connecting it with things at the salon, like my schedule or appointments feeling like they were being messed with. I remember feeling pretty sure someone there was interfering with it, like not just a mistake, but on purpose.\n\nSo, yeah… if you mean hearing the voice while I wasn’t depressed most of the time, then yes. There was a period like that, and it lasted several weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice and the feeling that people at the salon were interfering with your schedule been going on for at least 6 months?\n\nPatient: Yes… it’s definitely been more than six months. It’s been, I think, about a year and four months now since the voice started happening and since I started feeling like someone was targeting my bookings or messing with my schedule at the salon.\n\nIt’s hard to say the exact date because it didn’t feel like one clear moment where everything suddenly started. It was more like little things at first, like I’d notice something off with the appointments or I’d hear her voice and then I’d start putting it together. But if I’m being honest, this has been going on for around a year and four months, not just a few months.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been doing noticeably worse overall at work or in taking care of yourself than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I mean, I don’t like admitting that, because I used to be really reliable at work. Like, I was the person who remembered little things for clients, kept my station clean, stayed on top of timing, all of that. But since this started, I’ve definitely been doing worse.\n\nAt the salon, I make mistakes with really basic routines now, things I shouldn’t mess up—like mixing up small steps, forgetting what I already set out, taking too long to finish things that used to feel automatic. I’m slower with clients, and then I get nervous because I can tell they notice. My manager has lost trust in me, I think. She doesn’t say it in a cruel way, but she checks on me more, or hesitates before giving me certain appointments, and that feels awful because I know I wasn’t like that before.\n\nAnd with clients too… some of them don’t book with me as much, or they seem less comfortable. I’ve canceled things I used to always handle, even simple appointments or helping with cleanup or extra tasks. I used to be dependable, and now I feel like people don’t really count on me the same way.\n\nOutside of work, I’ve pulled away from friends a lot. I don’t answer messages like I used to, and I don’t really go out. I also stopped showing up for the volunteer haircuts I used to do at the senior center, which I feel guilty about, because I actually cared about that. It’s just like… overall I’m not functioning the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself talking much less than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t know if I noticed it at first, because I’m still technically talking at work when I have to, like asking what someone wants done with their hair or answering a basic question. But compared to how I used to be, it’s really different.\n\nI used to chat with clients the whole appointment sometimes—about their kids, vacations, shows, just little normal salon stuff. I’d remember details and ask follow-up questions, and it came naturally. I also used to help the newer stylists, like if they were nervous or unsure about something, I’d step in and talk them through it. I liked being that person.\n\nNow I mostly keep my head down. I say what I need to say, but I don’t really start conversations. If a client is quiet, I’m almost relieved because then I don’t have to pretend I have that energy. And even with friends, I used to go hiking and talk for hours, or make plans, or just send dumb messages during the day. Now I avoid people more. It’s not that I hate them or anything, I just… I don’t have that spark in me. Talking feels like it takes effort I don’t really have.\n\nDoctor: Have other people commented that your face looks less expressive or that your voice sounds flatter than it used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if anyone has said it exactly like that, like “your face looks flat” or “your voice is monotone.” Not in those words, at least. But people have definitely noticed that I’m not as… animated, I guess.\n\nAt work, I used to be really chatty with clients. Like I’d smile more, ask them about their lives, remember little things, joke around a bit, and it felt natural. I’d also help the newer stylists and kind of talk them through things if they were nervous. Now I mostly keep my head down and just say what I have to say to get through the appointment. So even if nobody says, “your face looks less expressive,” I can tell they feel the difference.\n\nFriends have noticed too. I used to go hiking with them and talk a lot, or make plans and actually sound excited. Now I avoid people more, and I think I probably do sound flatter because I just don’t have that spark in me like I used to. It’s not like I’m trying to be cold or rude. It just takes so much effort to seem normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s kind of embarrassing to say because some of the things I used to enjoy were such normal parts of who I was, and now they feel like chores or like I’m watching somebody else do them from far away.\n\nAt work, I used to actually enjoy talking with clients. Not just the haircut part, but the whole back-and-forth—asking about their family, remembering some little thing they told me last time, joking around, making them feel comfortable. I liked helping the newer stylists too, like if someone was nervous or unsure, I’d step in and talk them through it. That used to make me feel useful, like I had a place there.\n\nNow I mostly just keep my head down and try to get through the appointment. I don’t really start conversations unless I have to. If someone wants to chat, I feel this pressure to respond the right way, and I just don’t have that same spark in me. It’s not that I suddenly dislike them, it’s more like the interest or energy that used to come naturally just isn’t there.\n\nAnd with friends, yeah… I used to go hiking and actually look forward to it. I’d talk the whole time, make plans, take pictures, send messages afterward. Now I avoid people a lot more. Even if someone invites me, I’ll put off answering because the idea of going and acting like myself feels exhausting. So yes, I’ve lost interest, or maybe lost the ability to feel interested the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I’ve had plenty of nights where I didn’t sleep well, or I stayed up too late and then had to drag myself through the next day, but that’s different from feeling full of energy.\n\nIf you mean like sleeping only a few hours and then waking up feeling amazing or unstoppable or like I didn’t need sleep at all… I don’t think so. At least nothing that stands out to me that way. Usually if I get less sleep, I feel it. I get tired, slower, more worn down. It’s not like I suddenly have all this extra energy.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a head injury, seizure, or another neurological illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve bumped my head like anyone has, probably, but not anything I’d call a real head injury or anything where I had to be treated for it.\n\nNo seizures that I know of. And I haven’t been told I have some neurological illness or anything like that. I guess I’m not totally sure what all counts as neurological, but nothing comes to mind—no diagnosis or major event like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using methamphetamine, cocaine, or any other drugs around the times when you hear the woman’s voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, no, not that I can connect to it. I haven’t noticed it being tied to anything like that.\n\nWhen it happens, I don’t sit there thinking, “Oh, this is because I took something,” or anything like that. I guess I’d say I’m not aware of using meth or cocaine around those times. I’m trying to think carefully because I don’t want to answer wrong, but there isn’t some pattern I can point to where I hear her voice after using drugs.\n\nI’m sorry if that’s not a very clear answer. It’s just… from what I can tell, no, I haven’t noticed drug use being part of when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting several days when you felt unusually euphoric or irritable?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I’ve definitely been irritable for several days before, but I don’t know if I’d call it unusual in the way you mean. Like, if I’m stressed or overwhelmed, I can get snappy or kind of on edge for a while, but it doesn’t feel like some totally different version of me, if that makes sense.\n\nAs far as euphoric… I don’t really think so. I don’t remember a stretch of several days where I was just unusually happy or high or anything like that. I’ve had good moods, obviously, and times where I felt better than usual, but not in a way that seemed extreme or like other people were concerned.\n\nI guess I’m trying to separate normal mood stuff from what you’re asking. If you mean several days where I was so happy or so irritable that it really stood out as abnormal, I can’t think of a clear time like that.\n\nDoctor: Does the woman’s voice ever say things that are not about you ruining things or being worthless?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s about that… about me ruining things, or being worthless, or like I’m messing everything up. That’s the main thing I hear.\n\nIt’ll happen at work sometimes when I’m doing really normal stuff, like sweeping up hair or rinsing out color bowls, and I’ll hear this woman’s voice kind of near the shampoo stations. And it feels like it’s nearby, not like I’m just thinking it. She’ll say I’m ruining everything, or that I can’t do anything right. Sometimes the salon is empty, or there’s nobody close enough to be saying it, which is what makes it so upsetting.\n\nI’m trying to think if she says other things… but honestly, I don’t think there’s a lot outside of that theme. It’s usually critical, like about me being worthless or messing everything up.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I mean… yes, kind of. I know this is going to sound strange, and I don’t really like saying it out loud because it makes me feel like I’m being dramatic, but I’ve had this feeling that someone at the salon is watching my bookings and messing with my hours on purpose.

I know it may not make sense from the outside, but when it keeps lining up that way, it’s hard for me not to think it’s on purpose.

Like there’s this drag on everything.\n\nAnd it’s not just me being picky about my work. Other people have started noticing too, which is honestly embarrassing. I’ll be near the shampoo stations, and I’ll hear this woman’s voice, like she’s close by, saying that I’m ruining everything. It makes me feel really unsettled, like I don’t trust the room for a minute. I remember feeling really sure about it, like shifts or appointments were being moved around or handled in a way that was meant to throw me off. And the voice would make it feel even more real, because I’d hear her saying things and then I’d start thinking, “Okay, see, something is happening here.”\n\nSo… yeah. It wasn’t only when I was depressed. It started, or at least it was happening, before the depression really took over.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least 2 weeks when you were hearing the woman’s voice even though you were not feeling depressed most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think it was definitely at least two weeks. It wasn’t just like one weird day or a couple of days. Before the depression really got bad, there was this stretch of several weeks where I was still mostly doing my normal routine—getting up, going into the salon, doing clients, cleaning up, answering people, like… functioning more normally, I guess.\n\nI wasn’t feeling happy, exactly, but I also wasn’t spending most of the time in that really heavy depressed place yet. And during that time, I was already hearing her voice sometimes. It would happen at work, and then I’d start connecting it with things at the salon, like my schedule or appointments feeling like they were being messed with. I remember feeling pretty sure someone there was interfering with it, like not just a mistake, but on purpose.\n\nSo, yeah… if you mean hearing the voice while I wasn’t depressed most of the time, then yes. There was a period like that, and it lasted several weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice and the feeling that people at the salon were interfering with your schedule been going on for at least 6 months?\n\nPatient: Yes… it’s definitely been more than six months. It’s been, I think, about a year and four months now since the voice started happening and since I started feeling like someone was targeting my bookings or messing with my schedule at the salon.\n\nIt’s hard to say the exact date because it didn’t feel like one clear moment where everything suddenly started. It was more like little things at first, like I’d notice something off with the appointments or I’d hear her voice and then I’d start putting it together. But if I’m being honest, this has been going on for around a year and four months, not just a few months.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been doing noticeably worse overall at work or in taking care of yourself than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I mean, I don’t like admitting that, because I used to be really reliable at work. Like, I was the person who remembered little things for clients, kept my station clean, stayed on top of timing, all of that. I’m slower with clients, and then I get nervous because I can tell they notice. My manager has lost trust in me, I think. She doesn’t say it in a cruel way, but she checks on me more, or hesitates before giving me certain appointments, and that feels awful because I know I wasn’t like that before.\n\nAnd with clients too… some of them don’t book with me as much, or they seem less comfortable. I’ve canceled things I used to always handle, even simple appointments or helping with cleanup or extra tasks. I used to be dependable, and now I feel like people don’t really count on me the same way.\n\nOutside of work, I’ve pulled away from friends a lot. I don’t answer messages like I used to, and I don’t really go out. I also stopped showing up for the volunteer haircuts I used to do at the senior center, which I feel guilty about, because I actually cared about that. It’s just like… overall I’m not functioning the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself talking much less than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t know if I noticed it at first, because I’m still technically talking at work when I have to, like asking what someone wants done with their hair or answering a basic question. I’d remember details and ask follow-up questions, and it came naturally. I also used to help the newer stylists, like if they were nervous or unsure about something, I’d step in and talk them through it. I liked being that person.\n\nNow I mostly keep my head down. I say what I need to say, but I don’t really start conversations. If a client is quiet, I’m almost relieved because then I don’t have to pretend I have that energy. And even with friends, I used to go hiking and talk for hours, or make plans, or just send dumb messages during the day. Now I avoid people more. It’s not that I hate them or anything, I just… I don’t have that spark in me. Talking feels like it takes effort I don’t really have.\n\nDoctor: Have other people commented that your face looks less expressive or that your voice sounds flatter than it used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if anyone has said it exactly like that, like “your face looks flat” or “your voice is monotone.” Not in those words, at least. But people have definitely noticed that I’m not as… animated, I guess.\n\nAt work, I used to be really chatty with clients. Like I’d smile more, ask them about their lives, remember little things, joke around a bit, and it felt natural. I’d also help the newer stylists and kind of talk them through things if they were nervous. Now I mostly keep my head down and just say what I have to say to get through the appointment. I used to go hiking with them and talk a lot, or make plans and actually sound excited. Now I avoid people more, and I think I probably do sound flatter because I just don’t have that spark in me like I used to. It’s not like I’m trying to be cold or rude. It just takes so much effort to seem normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. Not just the haircut part, but the whole back-and-forth—asking about their family, remembering some little thing they told me last time, joking around, making them feel comfortable. I liked helping the newer stylists too, like if someone was nervous or unsure, I’d step in and talk them through it. That used to make me feel useful, like I had a place there.\n\nNow I mostly just keep my head down and try to get through the appointment. I don’t really start conversations unless I have to. I’d talk the whole time, make plans, take pictures, send messages afterward. Now I avoid people a lot more. I know this is going to sound strange, and I don’t really like saying it out loud because it makes me feel like I’m being dramatic, but I’ve had this feeling that someone at the salon is watching my bookings and messing with my hours on purpose.\n\nLike, I’ll talk about needing to save money, or how I’m trying to get ahead on bills, and then right after that it feels like my schedule suddenly gets weirdly slow. Not just normal slow, but like the days I need clients the most, those are the days where my bookings disappear or I don’t get put on the right hours. And I start thinking, how would that happen unless someone was interfering?\n\nIt doesn’t feel exactly like people are trying to physically hurt me or anything, but it does feel like someone is trying to block me or mess with me, like they don’t want me to do well. I know it may not make sense from the outside, but when it keeps lining up that way, it’s hard for me not to think it’s on purpose.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt that your thoughts were being controlled by someone else?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 21 / hairdresser_hairstylist_or_cosmetologist / associates\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Delusions\nmanifestation: \"I know it sounds strange, but I’m pretty sure someone at the salon has been watching my bookings and messing with my hours on purpose, because the slow days seem to happen right after I talk about saving money.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Verbose patient.\nA verbose patient may\n1) provide excessively detailed responses when describing their symptoms and experiences, even when the details are directly relevant,\n2) elaborate extensively on the background, events, thoughts, feelings, and personal experiences surrounding their symptoms, and\n3) demonstrate difficulty allowing the doctor to guide the interview or move on to another clinical topic.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
I don’t know if I’d say controlled exactly, like someone putting thoughts into my head or moving them around like a remote control… that doesn’t feel like the right way to describe it.

It’s more that I get really convinced someone at the salon is interfering with things around me. Like my bookings and my hours. I know that’s not exactly what you asked, but that’s the closest thing I can think of. I’ll mention needing to save money or trying to catch up on bills, and then suddenly my schedule gets slow in this way that feels too specific. Like the slow days happen right after I talk about it, and it makes me feel like someone has been watching my bookings and messing with my hours on purpose.

So not my thoughts being controlled, no… but I do feel like someone is kind of messing with my life or trying to block me, if that makes sense.

Before the depression really got bad, there was this stretch of several weeks where I was still mostly doing my normal routine—getting up, going into the salon, doing clients, cleaning up, answering people, like… functioning more normally, I guess.\n\nI wasn’t feeling happy, exactly, but I also wasn’t spending most of the time in that really heavy depressed place yet. And during that time, I was already hearing her voice sometimes. It would happen at work, and then I’d start connecting it with things at the salon, like my schedule or appointments feeling like they were being messed with. It’s been, I think, about a year and four months now since the voice started happening and since I started feeling like someone was targeting my bookings or messing with my schedule at the salon.\n\nIt’s hard to say the exact date because it didn’t feel like one clear moment where everything suddenly started. It was more like little things at first, like I’d notice something off with the appointments or I’d hear her voice and then I’d start putting it together. But if I’m being honest, this has been going on for around a year and four months, not just a few months.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been doing noticeably worse overall at work or in taking care of yourself than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I mean, I don’t like admitting that, because I used to be really reliable at work. Like, I was the person who remembered little things for clients, kept my station clean, stayed on top of timing, all of that. But since this started, I’ve definitely been doing worse.\n\nAt the salon, I make mistakes with really basic routines now, things I shouldn’t mess up—like mixing up small steps, forgetting what I already set out, taking too long to finish things that used to feel automatic. I’m slower with clients, and then I get nervous because I can tell they notice. My manager has lost trust in me, I think. She doesn’t say it in a cruel way, but she checks on me more, or hesitates before giving me certain appointments, and that feels awful because I know I wasn’t like that before.\n\nAnd with clients too… some of them don’t book with me as much, or they seem less comfortable. I’ve canceled things I used to always handle, even simple appointments or helping with cleanup or extra tasks. I used to be dependable, and now I feel like people don’t really count on me the same way.\n\nOutside of work, I’ve pulled away from friends a lot. I don’t answer messages like I used to, and I don’t really go out. I also stopped showing up for the volunteer haircuts I used to do at the senior center, which I feel guilty about, because I actually cared about that. It’s just like… overall I’m not functioning the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself talking much less than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t know if I noticed it at first, because I’m still technically talking at work when I have to, like asking what someone wants done with their hair or answering a basic question. But compared to how I used to be, it’s really different.\n\nI used to chat with clients the whole appointment sometimes—about their kids, vacations, shows, just little normal salon stuff. I’d remember details and ask follow-up questions, and it came naturally. I also used to help the newer stylists, like if they were nervous or unsure about something, I’d step in and talk them through it. I liked being that person.\n\nNow I mostly keep my head down. I say what I need to say, but I don’t really start conversations. If a client is quiet, I’m almost relieved because then I don’t have to pretend I have that energy. And even with friends, I used to go hiking and talk for hours, or make plans, or just send dumb messages during the day. Now I avoid people more. It’s not that I hate them or anything, I just… I don’t have that spark in me. Talking feels like it takes effort I don’t really have.\n\nDoctor: Have other people commented that your face looks less expressive or that your voice sounds flatter than it used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if anyone has said it exactly like that, like “your face looks flat” or “your voice is monotone.” Not in those words, at least. But people have definitely noticed that I’m not as… animated, I guess.\n\nAt work, I used to be really chatty with clients. Like I’d smile more, ask them about their lives, remember little things, joke around a bit, and it felt natural. I’d also help the newer stylists and kind of talk them through things if they were nervous. Now I mostly keep my head down and just say what I have to say to get through the appointment. So even if nobody says, “your face looks less expressive,” I can tell they feel the difference.\n\nFriends have noticed too. I used to go hiking with them and talk a lot, or make plans and actually sound excited. Now I avoid people more, and I think I probably do sound flatter because I just don’t have that spark in me like I used to. It’s not like I’m trying to be cold or rude. It just takes so much effort to seem normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s kind of embarrassing to say because some of the things I used to enjoy were such normal parts of who I was, and now they feel like chores or like I’m watching somebody else do them from far away.\n\nAt work, I used to actually enjoy talking with clients. Not just the haircut part, but the whole back-and-forth—asking about their family, remembering some little thing they told me last time, joking around, making them feel comfortable. I liked helping the newer stylists too, like if someone was nervous or unsure, I’d step in and talk them through it. That used to make me feel useful, like I had a place there.\n\nNow I mostly just keep my head down and try to get through the appointment. I don’t really start conversations unless I have to. If someone wants to chat, I feel this pressure to respond the right way, and I just don’t have that same spark in me. It’s not that I suddenly dislike them, it’s more like the interest or energy that used to come naturally just isn’t there.\n\nAnd with friends, yeah… I used to go hiking and actually look forward to it. I’d talk the whole time, make plans, take pictures, send messages afterward. Now I avoid people a lot more. Even if someone invites me, I’ll put off answering because the idea of going and acting like myself feels exhausting. So yes, I’ve lost interest, or maybe lost the ability to feel interested the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I mean… I’ve had plenty of nights where I didn’t sleep well, or I stayed up too late and then had to drag myself through the next day, but that’s different from feeling full of energy.\n\nIf you mean like sleeping only a few hours and then waking up feeling amazing or unstoppable or like I didn’t need sleep at all… I don’t think so. At least nothing that stands out to me that way. Usually if I get less sleep, I feel it. I get tired, slower, more worn down. It’s not like I suddenly have all this extra energy.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a head injury, seizure, or another neurological illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve bumped my head like anyone has, probably, but not anything I’d call a real head injury or anything where I had to be treated for it.\n\nNo seizures that I know of. And I haven’t been told I have some neurological illness or anything like that. I guess I’m not totally sure what all counts as neurological, but nothing comes to mind—no diagnosis or major event like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using methamphetamine, cocaine, or any other drugs around the times when you hear the woman’s voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, no, not that I can connect to it. I haven’t noticed it being tied to anything like that.\n\nWhen it happens, I don’t sit there thinking, “Oh, this is because I took something,” or anything like that. I guess I’d say I’m not aware of using meth or cocaine around those times. I’m trying to think carefully because I don’t want to answer wrong, but there isn’t some pattern I can point to where I hear her voice after using drugs.\n\nI’m sorry if that’s not a very clear answer. It’s just… from what I can tell, no, I haven’t noticed drug use being part of when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting several days when you felt unusually euphoric or irritable?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I’ve definitely been irritable for several days before, but I don’t know if I’d call it unusual in the way you mean. Like, if I’m stressed or overwhelmed, I can get snappy or kind of on edge for a while, but it doesn’t feel like some totally different version of me, if that makes sense.\n\nAs far as euphoric… I don’t really think so. I don’t remember a stretch of several days where I was just unusually happy or high or anything like that. I’ve had good moods, obviously, and times where I felt better than usual, but not in a way that seemed extreme or like other people were concerned.\n\nI guess I’m trying to separate normal mood stuff from what you’re asking. If you mean several days where I was so happy or so irritable that it really stood out as abnormal, I can’t think of a clear time like that.\n\nDoctor: Does the woman’s voice ever say things that are not about you ruining things or being worthless?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s about that… about me ruining things, or being worthless, or like I’m messing everything up. That’s the main thing I hear.\n\nIt’ll happen at work sometimes when I’m doing really normal stuff, like sweeping up hair or rinsing out color bowls, and I’ll hear this woman’s voice kind of near the shampoo stations. And it feels like it’s nearby, not like I’m just thinking it. She’ll say I’m ruining everything, or that I can’t do anything right. Sometimes the salon is empty, or there’s nobody close enough to be saying it, which is what makes it so upsetting.\n\nI’m trying to think if she says other things… but honestly, I don’t think there’s a lot outside of that theme. It’s usually critical, like about me being worthless or messing everything up.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that people were trying to harm you or interfere with you in a way that was not about you being worthless or ruining things?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, kind of. I know this is going to sound strange, and I don’t really like saying it out loud because it makes me feel like I’m being dramatic, but I’ve had this feeling that someone at the salon is watching my bookings and messing with my hours on purpose.\n\nLike, I’ll talk about needing to save money, or how I’m trying to get ahead on bills, and then right after that it feels like my schedule suddenly gets weirdly slow. Not just normal slow, but like the days I need clients the most, those are the days where my bookings disappear or I don’t get put on the right hours. And I start thinking, how would that happen unless someone was interfering?\n\nIt doesn’t feel exactly like people are trying to physically hurt me or anything, but it does feel like someone is trying to block me or mess with me, like they don’t want me to do well. I know it may not make sense from the outside, but when it keeps lining up that way, it’s hard for me not to think it’s on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being controlled by someone else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say controlled exactly, like someone putting thoughts into my head or moving them around like a remote control… that doesn’t feel like the right way to describe it.\n\nIt’s more that I get really convinced someone at the salon is interfering with things around me. Like my bookings and my hours. I know that’s not exactly what you asked, but that’s the closest thing I can think of. I’ll mention needing to save money or trying to catch up on bills, and then suddenly my schedule gets slow in this way that feels too specific. Like the slow days happen right after I talk about it, and it makes me feel like someone has been watching my bookings and messing with my hours on purpose.\n\nSo not my thoughts being controlled, no… but I do feel like someone is kind of messing with my life or trying to block me, if that makes sense.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Yes… when the sadness got really heavy, I was still hearing it. It wasn’t like the voice was only happening when I was feeling okay and then the depression came later, or the other way around. They were sort of happening at the same time.

Like, when I was in that really low place where everything felt heavy and stuck, I would still hear the woman’s voice around the shampoo stations, saying those things about me ruining everything or being worthless. And at the same time, I was also getting really caught up in the idea that someone was messing with my bookings or my hours on purpose. So it all kind of blended together — the depression, the voice, and feeling like someone was trying to sabotage me at work.

It didn’t feel separate in my head. It felt like while I was already really down, those things were also going on, which made it feel even harder to get out of.

What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: I guess… the main thing is I’ve just gotten really slow, like noticeably slow. I do hair, and at work it’s like my hands don’t do what they used to do. Even little things that were automatic before—sectioning someone’s hair, getting foils ready, mixing color—feel like I’m moving my hands through glue or something. Like there’s this drag on everything.\n\nAnd it’s not just me being picky about my work. Other people have started noticing too, which is honestly embarrassing. I used to be pretty quick, or at least normal, and now I’ll realize I’m taking forever on something simple. I’ll be standing there trying to get through a service, and it feels like my body is just… delayed. That’s kind of what made me think I should come in, because it’s affecting my job and people can tell I’m not moving like I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any shaking or tremor in your hands?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… not like a shaking, exactly. I haven’t noticed my hands trembling the way I’d imagine a tremor would look. It’s more that they feel slow or heavy when I’m trying to use them, but I can’t say I’ve seen them visibly shake. If there is any tiny movement, I haven’t really picked up on it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling depressed lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. Pretty much most days lately, honestly. It’s not even always like crying or some big dramatic thing, it’s more like I wake up and there’s already this heaviness there, like this flat sadness sitting in my chest before anything has even happened. \n\nAnd then I start thinking about my life, and it’s like… I had this picture of what I was trying to build, you know? Work, getting better at hair, maybe having my own place feel more settled, just normal things. But lately it feels like there’s no point in trying to build any of it, like I’m already behind or like it’s not going to turn into anything. It’s hard to explain without sounding really negative, but that’s how it feels most mornings.\n\nDoctor: Have you been hearing or seeing things that other people don’t seem to?\n\nPatient: Um… yeah. Not seeing things, really, I don’t think. But hearing, yes… sometimes. I feel weird saying that out loud because I know how it sounds, but it’s happened enough that I can’t just pretend it’s nothing.\n\nIt’s mostly at work, like when I’m doing the boring cleanup stuff after a client—sweeping up hair, rinsing color bowls, wiping things down. I’ll be near the shampoo stations, and I’ll hear this woman’s voice, like she’s close by, saying that I’m ruining everything. It’s not like a thought in my own head exactly… it sounds more like someone nearby said it, so I’ll turn around or look over, but sometimes the salon is empty, or nobody is anywhere near me.\n\nAnd it’s embarrassing because I’ll kind of freeze for a second and check if someone came in or if one of the other girls said something, but there won’t be anyone there. It makes me feel really unsettled, like I don’t trust the room for a minute. It’s not happening every second or anything, but it’s happened more than once.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you felt unusually high, energized, or wired for days in a row?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. I don’t think I’ve had something like that in the way you mean, like being super energized or wired for days where I’m unstoppable or anything.\n\nI mean, there are times I can’t sleep well or my mind feels kind of busy, but I wouldn’t say I feel “high.” It’s not like I suddenly have tons of energy and feel amazing. If anything, lately I usually feel kind of weighed down. So… no, I don’t think I’ve had days in a row where I felt unusually up or wired.\n\nDoctor: Have you heard that woman’s voice at times when you were not feeling depressed?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I think so. I mean, yes, there was a stretch before the depression got as heavy as it is now where I was still kind of functioning normally. Like I was getting up, going to work, doing clients, keeping up with things more or less. I didn’t feel great, exactly, but it wasn’t that same heavy, hopeless feeling taking over every day yet.\n\nBut during that time, I was already hearing her voice sometimes. And it kind of went along with this feeling I had at the salon, like someone there was messing with my schedule or interfering with it somehow. I remember feeling really sure about it, like shifts or appointments were being moved around or handled in a way that was meant to throw me off. And the voice would make it feel even more real, because I’d hear her saying things and then I’d start thinking, “Okay, see, something is happening here.”\n\nSo… yeah. It wasn’t only when I was depressed. It started, or at least it was happening, before the depression really took over.\n\nDoctor: Was there ever a period of at least 2 weeks when you were hearing the woman’s voice even though you were not feeling depressed most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes, I think it was definitely at least two weeks. It wasn’t just like one weird day or a couple of days. Before the depression really got bad, there was this stretch of several weeks where I was still mostly doing my normal routine—getting up, going into the salon, doing clients, cleaning up, answering people, like… functioning more normally, I guess.\n\nI wasn’t feeling happy, exactly, but I also wasn’t spending most of the time in that really heavy depressed place yet. And during that time, I was already hearing her voice sometimes. It would happen at work, and then I’d start connecting it with things at the salon, like my schedule or appointments feeling like they were being messed with. I remember feeling pretty sure someone there was interfering with it, like not just a mistake, but on purpose.\n\nSo, yeah… if you mean hearing the voice while I wasn’t depressed most of the time, then yes. There was a period like that, and it lasted several weeks.\n\nDoctor: Have the voice and the feeling that people at the salon were interfering with your schedule been going on for at least 6 months?\n\nPatient: Yes… it’s definitely been more than six months. It’s been, I think, about a year and four months now since the voice started happening and since I started feeling like someone was targeting my bookings or messing with my schedule at the salon.\n\nIt’s hard to say the exact date because it didn’t feel like one clear moment where everything suddenly started. It was more like little things at first, like I’d notice something off with the appointments or I’d hear her voice and then I’d start putting it together. But if I’m being honest, this has been going on for around a year and four months, not just a few months.\n\nDoctor: Since this started, have you been doing noticeably worse overall at work or in taking care of yourself than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… yes. I mean, I don’t like admitting that, because I used to be really reliable at work. Like, I was the person who remembered little things for clients, kept my station clean, stayed on top of timing, all of that. But since this started, I’ve definitely been doing worse.\n\nAt the salon, I make mistakes with really basic routines now, things I shouldn’t mess up—like mixing up small steps, forgetting what I already set out, taking too long to finish things that used to feel automatic. I’m slower with clients, and then I get nervous because I can tell they notice. My manager has lost trust in me, I think. She doesn’t say it in a cruel way, but she checks on me more, or hesitates before giving me certain appointments, and that feels awful because I know I wasn’t like that before.\n\nAnd with clients too… some of them don’t book with me as much, or they seem less comfortable. I’ve canceled things I used to always handle, even simple appointments or helping with cleanup or extra tasks. I used to be dependable, and now I feel like people don’t really count on me the same way.\n\nOutside of work, I’ve pulled away from friends a lot. I don’t answer messages like I used to, and I don’t really go out. I also stopped showing up for the volunteer haircuts I used to do at the senior center, which I feel guilty about, because I actually cared about that. It’s just like… overall I’m not functioning the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself talking much less than you used to?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. I don’t know if I noticed it at first, because I’m still technically talking at work when I have to, like asking what someone wants done with their hair or answering a basic question. But compared to how I used to be, it’s really different.\n\nI used to chat with clients the whole appointment sometimes—about their kids, vacations, shows, just little normal salon stuff. I’d remember details and ask follow-up questions, and it came naturally. I also used to help the newer stylists, like if they were nervous or unsure about something, I’d step in and talk them through it. I liked being that person.\n\nNow I mostly keep my head down. I say what I need to say, but I don’t really start conversations. If a client is quiet, I’m almost relieved because then I don’t have to pretend I have that energy. And even with friends, I used to go hiking and talk for hours, or make plans, or just send dumb messages during the day. Now I avoid people more. It’s not that I hate them or anything, I just… I don’t have that spark in me. Talking feels like it takes effort I don’t really have.\n\nDoctor: Have other people commented that your face looks less expressive or that your voice sounds flatter than it used to?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if anyone has said it exactly like that, like “your face looks flat” or “your voice is monotone.” Not in those words, at least. But people have definitely noticed that I’m not as… animated, I guess.\n\nAt work, I used to be really chatty with clients. Like I’d smile more, ask them about their lives, remember little things, joke around a bit, and it felt natural. I’d also help the newer stylists and kind of talk them through things if they were nervous. Now I mostly keep my head down and just say what I have to say to get through the appointment. So even if nobody says, “your face looks less expressive,” I can tell they feel the difference.\n\nFriends have noticed too. I used to go hiking with them and talk a lot, or make plans and actually sound excited. Now I avoid people more, and I think I probably do sound flatter because I just don’t have that spark in me like I used to. It’s not like I’m trying to be cold or rude. It just takes so much effort to seem normal.\n\nDoctor: Have you lost interest in things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah… I have. It’s kind of embarrassing to say because some of the things I used to enjoy were such normal parts of who I was, and now they feel like chores or like I’m watching somebody else do them from far away.\n\nAt work, I used to actually enjoy talking with clients. Not just the haircut part, but the whole back-and-forth—asking about their family, remembering some little thing they told me last time, joking around, making them feel comfortable. I liked helping the newer stylists too, like if someone was nervous or unsure, I’d step in and talk them through it. That used to make me feel useful, like I had a place there.\n\nNow I mostly just keep my head down and try to get through the appointment. I don’t really start conversations unless I have to. If someone wants to chat, I feel this pressure to respond the right way, and I just don’t have that same spark in me. It’s not that I suddenly dislike them, it’s more like the interest or energy that used to come naturally just isn’t there.\n\nAnd with friends, yeah… I used to go hiking and actually look forward to it. I’d talk the whole time, make plans, take pictures, send messages afterward. Now I avoid people a lot more. Even if someone invites me, I’ll put off answering because the idea of going and acting like myself feels exhausting. So yes, I’ve lost interest, or maybe lost the ability to feel interested the way I used to.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period when you needed much less sleep than usual and still felt full of energy?\n\nPatient: I’m not really sure. At least nothing that stands out to me that way. Usually if I get less sleep, I feel it. I get tired, slower, more worn down. It’s not like I suddenly have all this extra energy.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a head injury, seizure, or another neurological illness?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so. I mean, I’ve bumped my head like anyone has, probably, but not anything I’d call a real head injury or anything where I had to be treated for it.\n\nNo seizures that I know of. And I haven’t been told I have some neurological illness or anything like that. I guess I’m not totally sure what all counts as neurological, but nothing comes to mind—no diagnosis or major event like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you been using methamphetamine, cocaine, or any other drugs around the times when you hear the woman’s voice?\n\nPatient: I don’t think so… I mean, no, not that I can connect to it. I haven’t noticed it being tied to anything like that.\n\nWhen it happens, I don’t sit there thinking, “Oh, this is because I took something,” or anything like that. I guess I’d say I’m not aware of using meth or cocaine around those times. I’m trying to think carefully because I don’t want to answer wrong, but there isn’t some pattern I can point to where I hear her voice after using drugs.\n\nI’m sorry if that’s not a very clear answer. It’s just… from what I can tell, no, I haven’t noticed drug use being part of when it happens.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a period lasting several days when you felt unusually euphoric or irritable?\n\nPatient: I’m not sure… I mean, I’ve definitely been irritable for several days before, but I don’t know if I’d call it unusual in the way you mean. Like, if I’m stressed or overwhelmed, I can get snappy or kind of on edge for a while, but it doesn’t feel like some totally different version of me, if that makes sense.\n\nAs far as euphoric… I don’t really think so. I don’t remember a stretch of several days where I was just unusually happy or high or anything like that. I’ve had good moods, obviously, and times where I felt better than usual, but not in a way that seemed extreme or like other people were concerned.\n\nI guess I’m trying to separate normal mood stuff from what you’re asking. If you mean several days where I was so happy or so irritable that it really stood out as abnormal, I can’t think of a clear time like that.\n\nDoctor: Does the woman’s voice ever say things that are not about you ruining things or being worthless?\n\nPatient: Mostly it’s about that… about me ruining things, or being worthless, or like I’m messing everything up. Sometimes the salon is empty, or there’s nobody close enough to be saying it, which is what makes it so upsetting.\n\nI’m trying to think if she says other things… but honestly, I don’t think there’s a lot outside of that theme. It’s usually critical, like about me being worthless or messing everything up.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a belief that people were trying to harm you or interfere with you in a way that was not about you being worthless or ruining things?\n\nPatient: I mean… yes, kind of. Not just normal slow, but like the days I need clients the most, those are the days where my bookings disappear or I don’t get put on the right hours. I know it may not make sense from the outside, but when it keeps lining up that way, it’s hard for me not to think it’s on purpose.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt that your thoughts were being controlled by someone else?\n\nPatient: I don’t know if I’d say controlled exactly, like someone putting thoughts into my head or moving them around like a remote control… that doesn’t feel like the right way to describe it.\n\nIt’s more that I get really convinced someone at the salon is interfering with things around me. I’ll mention needing to save money or trying to catch up on bills, and then suddenly my schedule gets slow in this way that feels too specific. Like the slow days happen right after I talk about it, and it makes me feel like someone has been watching my bookings and messing with my hours on purpose.\n\nSo not my thoughts being controlled, no… but I do feel like someone is kind of messing with my life or trying to block me, if that makes sense.\n\nDoctor: When you were in that heavy depressed state, were you still hearing the woman’s voice?\n\nPatient: Yes… when the sadness got really heavy, I was still hearing it.
